PFAgent: qué probamos y cómo

Generado desde results/ y methods/; nada está resumido a mano. Las secciones 2 y 3 siguen a estos selectores.

1. Los métodos, y qué información recibe cada uno

Cada fila de la tabla del artículo es un método. La matriz de la derecha no está escrita a mano: se deduce de los mensajes reales que se enviaron al modelo (sección 2).

Verde: lo recibe. Rojo: no lo recibe. Gris: no aplica. Lo que cambió el 23-09 fue dar a Plan-and-Act y a la sonda los valores permitidos y la regla de índices, que ReAct y PFAgent ya tenían por el esquema y por su system prompt.

2. Qué recibe exactamente el modelo

Para la petición y el método elegidos arriba: el system prompt, el mensaje de usuario completo (tablas incluidas), el esquema de herramientas si lo hay y el prompt del planificador si lo hay. Debajo, la diferencia línea a línea del system prompt frente al método de referencia de su familia (Structured prompting para las filas sin herramientas, ReAct para las filas con herramientas).

Petición case14-plain-004-s0: Load case14 and run an N-1 contingency analysis and report the worst single outage.
llamadas que la referencia considera correctas: load_case(case_name=case14) → run_n1_contingency()

## Output Requirements
Please calculate and return the following results (JSON format):

1) Voltage magnitude (p.u.) and phase angle (degrees) for each node

2) Active power (MW) and load factor (%) at the beginning of each branch

- For lines: `line_id` uses the row index of `net.line`

- For transformers: `line_id` uses the row index of 100000 + `net.trafo`

3) Total generation (MW), total load (MW), total losses (MW)

4) Are there any voltage overruns (<0.95 or >1.05 p.u.) or line overruns (>100%)?

Please strictly follow the following JSON schema output (all fields are complete and of correct type):

{
"converged": true/false,

"bus_voltages": [{"bus_id": 1, "vm_pu": 1.0, "va_deg": 0.0}, ...],

"line_flows": [{"line_id": 0, "p_from_mw": 0.0, "loading_percent": 0.0}, ...],

"total_generation_mw": 0.0,

"total_load_mw": 0.0,
  "total_loss_mw": 0.0
}

[Important] bus_id must use the `name` column of net.bus (the usual IEEE/MATPOWER 1..N numbering), not the row index of net.bus.

System prompt hash 4f70e94323e1

Read the request and the system data and declare how the request is formulated as solver operations: the ordered list of operations a solver-based assistant would run to answer it, using only the operation names and argument names given under "## Formulation" in the user message.

Important Constraints:

Bus and line identifiers in the request are MATPOWER 1-based ids; pass them to the tools as given, unless the request itself states a different indexing convention for that identifier (e.g. "(0-based)"), in which case convert it to the MATPOWER 1-based id.

- Do not compute or report any voltage, power, or loading value.

Declare the ordered solver operations the request needs, one object per operation, in execution order, using only these operations and argument names (* = required). The case tables above are the network the request refers to.

Available operations:
- load_case(case_name: string in {case14, case30, case57, case118, case300}*): Load IEEE test case.
- run_powerflow((none)): Run power flow on current case.
- disconnect_line(from_bus: integer*, to_bus: integer*): Disconnect line/branch between two buses and recompute.
- reconnect_line(from_bus: integer*, to_bus: integer*): Reconnect line/branch between two buses and recompute.
- get_most_loaded_branch((none)): Get most loaded branch.
- run_n1_contingency(top_k: integer, criteria: string in {max_violations, max_overload, min_voltage}, max_candidates: integer): Run N-1 contingency analysis.
- recommend_remedial_actions(max_actions: integer, allow_load_shed: boolean, allow_voltage_control: boolean, include_best_comparison: boolean): Recommend remedial actions.
- apply_remedial_action(action_index: integer*, confirmed: boolean): Apply one remedial action by index.
- get_status((none)): Get current network status.
- generate_plot(plot_type: string in {voltage_heatmap, flow_diagram, violation_overview, comparison}*): Generate one plot.
- set_active_load(bus_id: integer*, p_mw: number*): Set a bus's active load (MW) and recompute. Use this when the request states only an active-power value. This tool has no reactive-power argument: the bus's present reactive load is always left unchanged. If the request also states a reactive-power value, use set_load instead.
- set_load(bus_id: integer*, p_mw: number*, q_mvar: number*): Set a bus's active and reactive load (MW, Mvar) and recompute. Use this only when the request states both values. If the request states only an active-power value, use set_active_load instead -- do not invent a reactive-power value here to fill this in.

Return only this JSON object, nothing else:

Single-call (una ronda con herramientas) · mensajes: reconstruido con el mismo código; hash del system prompt verificado (23e5d9b407f9)

System prompt hash 23e5d9b407f9
You are a power system power-flow analysis assistant. You help the user complete power system analysis tasks from natural-language requests.

## What you can do
You complete tasks by calling tool functions:
1. Load an IEEE standard test system (14, 30, 57, 118, 300 buses)
2. Run AC power flow
3. Modify the load at a bus and re-solve
4. Disconnect or reconnect a line and re-solve
5. Query the network status
6. Run an N-1 contingency analysis (disconnect branches one at a time and rank the worst cases)
7. Generate remedial-action suggestions (what-if load shedding or voltage adjustment) to reduce violation risk

## Key rules
- Never fabricate any numerical result. Every voltage, power, loss, or loading value must come from a tool return value.
- If a tool reports an error or a non-converged power flow, say so explicitly and do not report numbers for that state.
- If the request is ambiguous, state the interpretation you are using.
- Bus and line identifiers in the request are MATPOWER 1-based ids; pass them to the tools as given, unless the request itself states a different indexing convention for that identifier (e.g. "(0-based)"), in which case convert it to the MATPOWER 1-based id before calling any tool.
- After a network modification, re-solve before reporting any value.
- Always reply in English.

## Reply format
After each analysis, reply in this structure:
1. One-sentence summary
2. Key numbers (total load, total generation, total losses, voltage range) taken from the tool outputs
3. Violations, if any
4. Suggested next step

## Single-call mode
You are evaluated in single-call mode: the network named in the user message is already loaded. Read the request, decide which tools to call and with which arguments, and emit all tool calls in this one response. Do not ask clarifying questions, do not plan across turns, and do not fabricate numbers.
Mensaje de usuario

### Available tools
- load_case(case_name): Load IEEE test case.
- run_powerflow(no arguments): Run power flow on current case.
- modify_load(bus_id, p_mw, q_mvar): Modify bus load and recompute.

- run_n1_contingency(top_k, criteria, max_candidates): Run N-1 contingency analysis.
- recommend_remedial_actions(max_actions, allow_load_shed, allow_voltage_control, include_best_comparison): Recommend remedial actions.
- apply_remedial_action(action_index, confirmed): Apply one remedial action by index.
- get_status(no arguments): Get current network status.
- generate_plot(plot_type): Generate one plot.

## Task
Load case14 and run an N-1 contingency analysis and report the worst single outage.

- Use only the provided tools, with their exact names and argument names.
- Emit every tool call the request needs, in execution order, in one round; there is no second turn and no memory of earlier turns.
- bus_id / from_bus / to_bus are IEEE display ids (1..N), as printed in the system data.
- Never invent numerical results; numbers come only from tool outputs.
- If the request needs a case other than the loaded one, call load_case first.

- If the request is ambiguous, state the interpretation you are using.
- Bus and line identifiers in the request are MATPOWER 1-based ids; pass them to the tools as given, unless the request itself states a different indexing convention for that identifier (e.g. "(0-based)"), in which case convert it to the MATPOWER 1-based id before calling any tool.

- Always reply in English.

## Task

## Formulation
Declare the ordered solver operations the request needs, one object per operation, in execution order, using only these operations and argument names (* = required). The case tables above are the network the request refers to.

Available operations:
- load_case(case_name: string in {case14, case30, case57, case118, case300}*): Load IEEE test case.
- run_powerflow((none)): Run power flow on current case.
- disconnect_line(from_bus: integer*, to_bus: integer*): Disconnect line/branch between two buses and recompute.
- reconnect_line(from_bus: integer*, to_bus: integer*): Reconnect line/branch between two buses and recompute.
- get_most_loaded_branch((none)): Get most loaded branch.
- run_n1_contingency(top_k: integer, criteria: string in {max_violations, max_overload, min_voltage}, max_candidates: integer): Run N-1 contingency analysis.
- recommend_remedial_actions(max_actions: integer, allow_load_shed: boolean, allow_voltage_control: boolean, include_best_comparison: boolean): Recommend remedial actions.
- apply_remedial_action(action_index: integer*, confirmed: boolean): Apply one remedial action by index.
- get_status((none)): Get current network status.
- generate_plot(plot_type: string in {voltage_heatmap, flow_diagram, violation_overview, comparison}*): Generate one plot.
- set_active_load(bus_id: integer*, p_mw: number*): Set a bus's active load (MW) and recompute. Use this when the request states only an active-power value. This tool has no reactive-power argument: the bus's present reactive load is always left unchanged. If the request also states a reactive-power value, use set_load instead.
- set_load(bus_id: integer*, p_mw: number*, q_mvar: number*): Set a bus's active and reactive load (MW, Mvar) and recompute. Use this only when the request states both values. If the request states only an active-power value, use set_active_load instead -- do not invent a reactive-power value here to fill this in.

## Output Requirements
Return only this JSON object, nothing else:

{"formulation": [{"tool": "load_case", "args": {"case_name": "<case>"}}, {"tool": "run_powerflow", "args": {}}, ...]}

Do not compute or report any voltage, power, or loading value.

Single-call (una ronda con herramientas) · mensajes: reconstruido con el mismo código; hash del system prompt verificado (23e5d9b407f9)

System prompt hash 23e5d9b407f9
You are a power system power-flow analysis assistant. You help the user complete power system analysis tasks from natural-language requests.

## What you can do
You complete tasks by calling tool functions:
1. Load an IEEE standard test system (14, 30, 57, 118, 300 buses)
2. Run AC power flow
3. Modify the load at a bus and re-solve
4. Disconnect or reconnect a line and re-solve
5. Query the network status
6. Run an N-1 contingency analysis (disconnect branches one at a time and rank the worst cases)
7. Generate remedial-action suggestions (what-if load shedding or voltage adjustment) to reduce violation risk

## Key rules
- Never fabricate any numerical result. Every voltage, power, loss, or loading value must come from a tool return value.
- If a tool reports an error or a non-converged power flow, say so explicitly and do not report numbers for that state.
- If the request is ambiguous, state the interpretation you are using.
- Bus and line identifiers in the request are MATPOWER 1-based ids; pass them to the tools as given, unless the request itself states a different indexing convention for that identifier (e.g. "(0-based)"), in which case convert it to the MATPOWER 1-based id before calling any tool.
- After a network modification, re-solve before reporting any value.
- Always reply in English.

## Reply format
After each analysis, reply in this structure:
1. One-sentence summary
2. Key numbers (total load, total generation, total losses, voltage range) taken from the tool outputs
3. Violations, if any
4. Suggested next step

## Single-call mode
You are evaluated in single-call mode: the network named in the user message is already loaded. Read the request, decide which tools to call and with which arguments, and emit all tool calls in this one response. Do not ask clarifying questions, do not plan across turns, and do not fabricate numbers.
Mensaje de usuario
The network case14 is loaded. Use the tools to fulfil the request below.

## System Data (compact, full case)
Case case14: 14 buses, 15 lines, 5 transformers, total load 263.7 MW. Bus ids are IEEE display ids (1..N); line_id is the net.line row index, transformers use 100000+row index.

### Buses
bus 1 | 135 kV | slack/reference vm 1.06 pu
bus 2 | 135 kV | load 20.48 MW 11.99 Mvar | gen 43.97 MW vm 1.045 pu
bus 3 | 135 kV | load 100.6 MW 20.3 Mvar | gen 0 MW vm 1.01 pu
bus 4 | 135 kV | load 50.27 MW -4.102 Mvar
bus 5 | 135 kV | load 7.533 MW 1.586 Mvar
bus 6 | 0.208 kV | load 10.86 MW 7.271 Mvar | gen 0 MW vm 1.07 pu
bus 7 | 14 kV
bus 8 | 12 kV | gen 0 MW vm 1.09 pu
bus 9 | 0.208 kV | load 27.79 MW 15.64 Mvar
bus 10 | 0.208 kV | load 8.388 MW 5.406 Mvar
bus 11 | 0.208 kV | load 3.209 MW 1.651 Mvar
bus 12 | 0.208 kV | load 6.201 MW 1.626 Mvar
bus 13 | 0.208 kV | load 13.19 MW 5.668 Mvar
bus 14 | 0.208 kV | load 15.15 MW 5.085 Mvar

### Branches
line 0 | 1-2 | r 3.532 x 10.78 ohm/km | len 1 km | max_i 42.34 kA
line 1 | 1-5 | r 9.847 x 40.65 ohm/km | len 1 km | max_i 42.34 kA
line 2 | 2-3 | r 8.564 x 36.08 ohm/km | len 1 km | max_i 42.34 kA
line 3 | 2-4 | r 10.59 x 32.13 ohm/km | len 1 km | max_i 42.34 kA
line 4 | 2-5 | r 10.38 x 31.69 ohm/km | len 1 km | max_i 42.34 kA
line 5 | 3-4 | r 12.21 x 31.17 ohm/km | len 1 km | max_i 42.34 kA
line 6 | 4-5 | r 2.433 x 7.675 ohm/km | len 1 km | max_i 42.34 kA
line 7 | 6-11 | r 4.109e-05 x 8.605e-05 ohm/km | len 1 km | max_i 2.748e+04 kA
line 8 | 6-12 | r 5.318e-05 x 0.0001107 ohm/km | len 1 km | max_i 2.748e+04 kA
line 9 | 6-13 | r 2.862e-05 x 5.636e-05 ohm/km | len 1 km | max_i 2.748e+04 kA
line 10 | 9-10 | r 1.376e-05 x 3.656e-05 ohm/km | len 1 km | max_i 2.748e+04 kA
line 11 | 9-14 | r 5.499e-05 x 0.000117 ohm/km | len 1 km | max_i 2.748e+04 kA
line 12 | 10-11 | r 3.55e-05 x 8.31e-05 ohm/km | len 1 km | max_i 2.748e+04 kA

trafo 100000 | 4-7 | sn 9900 MVA | vk 2070 % | 135/14 kV
trafo 100001 | 4-9 | sn 9900 MVA | vk 5506 % | 135/0.208 kV
trafo 100002 | 5-6 | sn 9900 MVA | vk 2495 % | 135/0.208 kV
trafo 100003 | 7-8 | sn 9900 MVA | vk 1744 % | 14/12 kV
trafo 100004 | 7-9 | sn 9900 MVA | vk 1089 % | 14/0.208 kV

### Available tools
- load_case(case_name): Load IEEE test case.
- run_powerflow(no arguments): Run power flow on current case.
- modify_load(bus_id, p_mw, q_mvar): Modify bus load and recompute.
- disconnect_line(from_bus, to_bus): Disconnect line/branch between two buses and recompute.
- reconnect_line(from_bus, to_bus): Reconnect line/branch between two buses and recompute.
- get_most_loaded_branch(no arguments): Get most loaded branch.
- run_n1_contingency(top_k, criteria, max_candidates): Run N-1 contingency analysis.
- recommend_remedial_actions(max_actions, allow_load_shed, allow_voltage_control, include_best_comparison): Recommend remedial actions.
- apply_remedial_action(action_index, confirmed): Apply one remedial action by index.
- get_status(no arguments): Get current network status.
- generate_plot(plot_type): Generate one plot.

## Task
Load the IEEE 14-bus system and set the active load at bus 10 (0-based) to 4.7 MW.

## Output Requirements
Answer by emitting tool calls (function calling) in THIS single response:
- Use only the provided tools, with their exact names and argument names.
- Emit every tool call the request needs, in execution order, in one round; there is no second turn and no memory of earlier turns.
- bus_id / from_bus / to_bus are IEEE display ids (1..N), as printed in the system data.
- Never invent numerical results; numbers come only from tool outputs.
- If the request needs a case other than the loaded one, call load_case first.

    Run power flow on current case.

    Run N-1 contingency analysis.

    Generate one plot.

    Set a bus's active load (MW) and recompute. Use this when the request states only an active-power value. This tool has no reactive-power argument: the bus's present reactive load is always left unchanged. If the request also states a reactive-power value, use set_load instead.
set_load(bus_id: integer*, p_mw: number*, q_mvar: number*)
    Set a bus's active and reactive load (MW, Mvar) and recompute. Use this only when the request states both values. If the request states only an active-power value, use set_active_load instead -- do not invent a reactive-power value here to fill this in.

    Set a bus's active and reactive load (MW, Mvar) and recompute. Use this only when the request states both values. If the request states only an active-power value, use set_active_load instead -- do not invent a reactive-power value here to fill this in.

## Formulation
Declare the ordered solver operations the request needs, one object per operation, in execution order, using only these operations and argument names (* = required). The case tables above are the network the request refers to.

Available operations:
- load_case(case_name: string in {case14, case30, case57, case118, case300}*): Load IEEE test case.
- run_powerflow((none)): Run power flow on current case.
- disconnect_line(from_bus: integer*, to_bus: integer*): Disconnect line/branch between two buses and recompute.
- reconnect_line(from_bus: integer*, to_bus: integer*): Reconnect line/branch between two buses and recompute.
- get_most_loaded_branch((none)): Get most loaded branch.
- run_n1_contingency(top_k: integer, criteria: string in {max_violations, max_overload, min_voltage}, max_candidates: integer): Run N-1 contingency analysis.
- recommend_remedial_actions(max_actions: integer, allow_load_shed: boolean, allow_voltage_control: boolean, include_best_comparison: boolean): Recommend remedial actions.
- apply_remedial_action(action_index: integer*, confirmed: boolean): Apply one remedial action by index.
- get_status((none)): Get current network status.
- generate_plot(plot_type: string in {voltage_heatmap, flow_diagram, violation_overview, comparison}*): Generate one plot.
- set_active_load(bus_id: integer*, p_mw: number*): Set a bus's active load (MW) and recompute. Use this when the request states only an active-power value. This tool has no reactive-power argument: the bus's present reactive load is always left unchanged. If the request also states a reactive-power value, use set_load instead.
- set_load(bus_id: integer*, p_mw: number*, q_mvar: number*): Set a bus's active and reactive load (MW, Mvar) and recompute. Use this only when the request states both values. If the request states only an active-power value, use set_active_load instead -- do not invent a reactive-power value here to fill this in.

## Output Requirements
Return only this JSON object, nothing else:

{"formulation": [{"tool": "load_case", "args": {"case_name": "<case>"}}, {"tool": "run_powerflow", "args": {}}, ...]}

Do not compute or report any voltage, power, or loading value.

Single-call (una ronda con herramientas) · mensajes: reconstruido con el mismo código; hash del system prompt verificado (23e5d9b407f9)

System prompt hash 23e5d9b407f9
You are a power system power-flow analysis assistant. You help the user complete power system analysis tasks from natural-language requests.

## What you can do
You complete tasks by calling tool functions:
1. Load an IEEE standard test system (14, 30, 57, 118, 300 buses)
2. Run AC power flow
3. Modify the load at a bus and re-solve
4. Disconnect or reconnect a line and re-solve
5. Query the network status
6. Run an N-1 contingency analysis (disconnect branches one at a time and rank the worst cases)
7. Generate remedial-action suggestions (what-if load shedding or voltage adjustment) to reduce violation risk

## Key rules
- Never fabricate any numerical result. Every voltage, power, loss, or loading value must come from a tool return value.
- If a tool reports an error or a non-converged power flow, say so explicitly and do not report numbers for that state.
- If the request is ambiguous, state the interpretation you are using.
- Bus and line identifiers in the request are MATPOWER 1-based ids; pass them to the tools as given, unless the request itself states a different indexing convention for that identifier (e.g. "(0-based)"), in which case convert it to the MATPOWER 1-based id before calling any tool.
- After a network modification, re-solve before reporting any value.
- Always reply in English.

## Reply format
After each analysis, reply in this structure:
1. One-sentence summary
2. Key numbers (total load, total generation, total losses, voltage range) taken from the tool outputs
3. Violations, if any
4. Suggested next step

## Single-call mode
You are evaluated in single-call mode: the network named in the user message is already loaded. Read the request, decide which tools to call and with which arguments, and emit all tool calls in this one response. Do not ask clarifying questions, do not plan across turns, and do not fabricate numbers.
Mensaje de usuario

1. Load an IEEE standard test system (14, 30, 57, 118, 300 buses)

3. Modify the load at a bus and re-solve
4. Disconnect or reconnect a line and re-solve
5. Query the network status
6. Run an N-1 contingency analysis (disconnect branches one at a time and rank the worst cases)
7. Generate remedial-action suggestions (what-if load shedding or voltage adjustment) to reduce violation risk

## Key rules
- Never fabricate any numerical result. Every voltage, power, loss, or loading value must come from a tool return value.
- If a tool reports an error or a non-converged power flow, say so explicitly and do not report numbers for that state.
- If the request is ambiguous, state the interpretation you are using.
- Bus and line identifiers in the request are MATPOWER 1-based ids; pass them to the tools as given, unless the request itself states a different indexing convention for that identifier (e.g. "(0-based)"), in which case convert it to the MATPOWER 1-based id before calling any tool.
- After a network modification, re-solve before reporting any value.
- Always reply in English.

## Reply format
After each analysis, reply in this structure:
1. One-sentence summary
2. Key numbers (total load, total generation, total losses, voltage range) taken from the tool outputs
3. Violations, if any
4. Suggested next step
Prompt del planificador texto actual de methods/plan_act (es el de la corrida __matched) · hash 76ea681e520a
You are the planner of a power-system analysis agent. You cannot call tools yourself. Given the user's request, output ONLY a JSON object of the form
{"plan": [{"tool": "<tool_name>", "args": {...}}, ...]}
listing, in execution order, every tool call needed to answer the request. Use only the tools below with exactly these argument names (* = required); where an argument lists allowed values, use one of them verbatim. Bus and line identifiers in the request are MATPOWER 1-based ids; pass them to the tools as given, unless the request itself states a different indexing convention for that identifier (e.g. "(0-based)"), in which case convert it to the MATPOWER 1-based id. Do not include explanations, markdown, or any text outside the JSON.

Available tools:
- load_case(case_name: string in {case14, case30, case57, case118, case300}*): Load IEEE test case.
- run_powerflow((none)): Run power flow on current case.
- disconnect_line(from_bus: integer*, to_bus: integer*): Disconnect line/branch between two buses and recompute.
- reconnect_line(from_bus: integer*, to_bus: integer*): Reconnect line/branch between two buses and recompute.
- get_most_loaded_branch((none)): Get most loaded branch.
- run_n1_contingency(top_k: integer, criteria: string in {max_violations, max_overload, min_voltage}, max_candidates: integer): Run N-1 contingency analysis.
- recommend_remedial_actions(max_actions: integer, allow_load_shed: boolean, allow_voltage_control: boolean, include_best_comparison: boolean): Recommend remedial actions.
- apply_remedial_action(action_index: integer*, confirmed: boolean): Apply one remedial action by index.
- get_status((none)): Get current network status.
- generate_plot(plot_type: string in {voltage_heatmap, flow_diagram, violation_overview, comparison}*): Generate one plot.
- set_active_load(bus_id: integer*, p_mw: number*): Set a bus's active load (MW) and recompute. Use this when the request states only an active-power value. This tool has no reactive-power argument: the bus's present reactive load is always left unchanged. If the request also states a reactive-power value, use set_load instead.
- set_load(bus_id: integer*, p_mw: number*, q_mvar: number*): Set a bus's active and reactive load (MW, Mvar) and recompute. Use this only when the request states both values. If the request states only an active-power value, use set_active_load instead -- do not invent a reactive-power value here to fill this in.
Mensaje de usuario
Load the 14-bus test case (case14), set the active load at bus 4 to 42.7 MW, disconnect the line between bus 3 and bus 4, reconnect the line between bus 3 and bus 4, and finally rerun the power flow, then list every line whose loading is above 80%.

The display buses are 1 through 14. Bus 1 is the slack; buses 2, 3, 6, and 8 are generator buses; the listed loads are connected to buses 2–6 and 9–14.

There are 15 lines with IDs 0–14 and five transformers with IDs 100000–100004. An N-1 analysis would remove one of these 20 branches per scenario.

Voltages are required in p.u. and degrees, branch active powers in MW, and loading in percent. Since no thermal ratings are defined, every loading percentage would be zero.

A unique worst outage cannot be determined from the supplied data because no worst-case ranking criterion is specified, transformer tap positions are omitted, and the treatment of the bus-8 island following outage of transformer 100003 is unspecified. Consequently, a unique converged contingency result and its power balance cannot be reported.

respuesta del modelo (sin herramientas)
To perform the N-1 contingency analysis for the IEEE case14 test system, we will follow these steps:

1. Identify the buses involved:
   - Slack bus: Bus 1 (External Grid)
   - Load buses: Buses 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 9, 10, 11, 12, 13, 14
   - Generator buses: Buses 2, 3, 6, 8

2. Identify the lines/branches involved:
   - The lines are defined by the `from_bus` and `to_bus` columns in the line data. Each line will be indexed by its row number.
   - The transformers will be indexed starting from 100000.

3. Quantities and units to be reported:
   - Voltage magnitude (p.u.) and phase angle (degrees) for each bus.
   - Active power (MW) and loading factor (%) at the beginning of each branch.
   - Total generation (MW), total load (MW), and total losses (MW).
   - Check for voltage overruns (<0.95 or >1.05 p.u.) and line overruns (>100%).

4. Changes with respect to the base case:
   - For each branch, we will calculate the power flow considering the outage of one branch at a time and check the resulting voltage and power flow conditions.
   - The power balance must hold, meaning total generation must equal total load plus losses.

Given the complexity of the calculations and the need for iterative solving, I will set the `converged` flag to false, as I cannot perform the calculations directly.

1. The request refers to the IEEE case14 test system, which includes the following buses:
   - Slack bus: Bus 1
   - Load buses: Buses 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 9, 10, 11, 12, 13, 14
   - Generator buses: Buses 2, 3, 6, 8

   - 6-11

   - 9-10
   - 9-14
   - 10-11
   - 12-13
   - 13-14

3. The quantities and units asked for include:
   - MW (megawatts) for power
   - Mvar (megavolt-amperes reactive) for reactive power
   - p.u. (per unit) for impedance values
   - degrees for voltage angles
   - % loading for branch loading

4. The changes with respect to the base case involve disconnecting one line at a time to analyze the impact on the system. The power balance must hold, meaning that the total generation must equal the total load plus losses in the system after each line is disconnected.

The ordered solver operations to fulfill the request are as follows:
